CI verifies the profile that is EMITTED (key present, relative, not the host's name) - not the behaviour. The behaviour was proven live twice, which no CI job repeats: container rebuilt from the committed profile, three host/container alternations, zero deletions. Relative, not absolute, after review: one absolute path is shared by every project in a container, and a probe showed project a importing a package only b declared. Existing containers must be recreated; profiles already scaffolded in consumer repos are not rewritten. gh#531: line anchors re-pointed to the tests they were recorded for (they had slid onto other tests as lines moved).<br>[super-fr:test_init_scaffold.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_init_scaffold.py#test_a_uv_profile_keeps_uvs_environment_out_of_the_bind_mount>) [super-fr:test_init_scaffold.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_init_scaffold.py#test_this_repos_own_uv_profiles_carry_it>) |

| When OpenCode loads the plugin twice (global install plus a project-local copy), an idle run still gets one continuation nudge per run position | [super-fr:2026-09-22-opencode-plugin-delivery.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/journals/debug/2026-09-22-opencode-plugin-delivery.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/packages/fr-opencode-plugin/test/idle.test.ts>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | gh#563 consequence: global delivery doubles the load wherever a repo also loads the plugin locally (super-fr itself). The once-per-position memory is shared via globalThis; bun test pins one send for two instances (red first: 2 sends).<br>[super-fr:idle.test.ts](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/packages/fr-opencode-plugin/test/idle.test.ts>) |
| An operator who runs scripts/install.sh with OpenCode gets super-fr's OpenCode plugin (edit gate + idle adapter) installed globally in a form OpenCode loads, and --uninstall removes exactly it | [super-fr:2026-09-22-opencode-plugin-delivery.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/journals/debug/2026-09-22-opencode-plugin-delivery.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_opencode_plugin_delivery.py>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_opencode_plugin_live.py>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | gh#563. Unit: deliver-opencode-plugin.sh behaviour (top-level loader + sources, wholesale replace, scoped uninstall) and install.sh wiring. Integration, CI (opencode-plugin-test job, opencode-ai@1.18.32, FR_REQUIRE_OPENCODE=1): the real binary registers the delivered loader, and a sentinel plugin loaded by OpenCode's own runtime imports it and gets a base-clone edit refused / an out-of-repo edit allowed. A mutation deleting one delivered module left registration green and failed the runtime probe. POST-MERGE WALK (2026-09-22, #571 Test Plan): scripts/install.sh re-run from main (fr 4.17.0) on an OpenCode 1.18.32 machine delivered the loader + 3 sources, and opencode debug config listed the loader. Then one model-driven session (github-copilot/gpt-5.6-luna) in a throwaway fr-enabled base clone outside super-fr, so only the global copy was loaded, asked for an edit: the model's apply_patch was refused with 'fr-isolation: edit to .../README.md blocked' and the file was unchanged. Control: the same prompt with opencode run --pure (no external plugins) in an identical repo applied the edit. The model-driven path through the DELIVERED copy is therefore live-proven; still live-only, so the CI level is unchanged.<br>[super-fr:test_opencode_plugin_delivery.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_opencode_plugin_delivery.py>) [super-fr:test_opencode_plugin_live.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_opencode_plugin_live.py>) |

## 10 · Multi-backend git hosting

| Acceptance | Origin | Levels | Automation | Evidence / notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| An operator can run fr apply against a GitLab-backed repo and see phases rendered/observed/diffed/applied as GitLab Issues with correctly-shaped GitLab labels (color, length) | [super-fr:2026-07-09-multi-backend-git-host-adapters-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-multi-backend-git-host-adapters-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_glab.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | skipped | LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-09-19 against a self-hosted GitLab (gh-486): fr apply rendered, observed, diffed and applied two phases as GitLab Issues with correctly-shaped labels (#-prefixed 6-hex, applied once), re-ran clean, and propagated a state change then re-converged. Transcript in the PR. The row was previously 'ci' for a capability broken FOUR independent ways, none findable by unit test: the missing mandatory ref parameter; the Issue web_url being /-/work_items/N, which fr stored and then refused to parse (crashing observe on every re-apply, on gitlab.com too); ensure_labels aborting the whole apply on a 409 for a label that already existed, a tolerance its own docstring had claimed since 2026-07-09; and the body diff never converging because GitLab strips the rendered trailing newline. 'skipped', NOT 'ci': CI cannot reach a private instance, so the live half is hand-run per-PR. The unit half now asserts the REQUEST and the real captured error vocabulary, so the arg-blind 'lambda args:' mocks that hid all of this are gone. LIVE 2026-09-24 (multi-backend P9.T1.S1), a self-hosted GitLab (host/user/project redacted; private scratch project, deleted after): every RealGlabClient operation fr apply/observe uses passed when driven directly (ensure_labels create+converge, colour round-trip, create_issue returning /-/work_items/N, view, label add/remove, comment, list_linked_prs via related_merge_requests, pr_status_by_url, file_exists/list_dir/read_file, close). related_merge_requests is eventually consistent (empty right after the MR, populated seconds later). NOT walked: an end-to-end `fr apply` on a GitLab-backed plan, so the status stays skipped.<br>[super-fr:test_glab.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_glab.py>) [super-fr:test_real_glabclient.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_real_glabclient.py>) [super-fr:test_hostclient.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_hostclient.py>) [super-fr:test__urls.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test__urls.py>) |
| An operator can run fr apply against a Gitea-backed repo and see phases rendered/observed/diffed/applied as Gitea Issues with correctly-shaped labels | [super-fr:2026-07-09-multi-backend-git-host-adapters-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-multi-backend-git-host-adapters-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_tea.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | RealTeaClient satisfies the GhClient Protocol (mypy-verified) and is reachable from every fr apply CLI command via hostclient.client_for, gated on fr._hosts.detect_backend. Unit-verified against Gitea's own live swagger-spec-confirmed shapes (Phase 3/4, 2026-07-09). Not yet proven against a live Gitea instance -- owed to the plan's back-loaded manual verification phase (Phase 9).<br>[super-fr:test_tea.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_tea.py>) [super-fr:test_real_teaclient.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_real_teaclient.py>) [super-fr:test_hostclient.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_hostclient.py>) |
| fr isolation up/status/down/gc resolves the default branch and checks PR/MR-merge state correctly against a GitLab- or Gitea-backed repo, with zero gh-specific calls | [super-fr:2026-07-09-multi-backend-git-host-adapters-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-multi-backend-git-host-adapters-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | _resolve_default_branch and _pr_from both branch on fr._hosts.detect_backend (glab/tea equivalents of the gh calls, kept as a lightweight parallel branch on the isolation lifecycle's own Runner seam rather than merged into GhClient -- see the design doc's reasoning). Gitea's _pr_from has no single-shot branch->PR query (verified during research) and falls back to a list-and-filter scan -- a real, bounded, documented degradation. Not yet proven against a live GitLab/Gitea instance -- owed to Phase 9's manual verification.<br>[super-fr:test_isolation.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation.py>) |
| The VK dispatch bridge polls PR/MR status and auto-closes tracking Issues correctly for a GitLab- or Gitea-backed phase | [super-fr:2026-07-09-multi-backend-git-host-adapters-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-multi-backend-git-host-adapters-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_pr_observe.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Fixed the two fr-vk gh-bypass call sites (pr_observe.py/pr_state.py shelled out to literal gh subprocesses, bypassing GhClient) and bridge_cli.py's hardcoded RealGhClient() (now resolved per-repo via hostclient.client_for, since the bridge can watch repos on different backends). Consolidated 5 duplicate card-title regexes into fr_vk._cardref, backward-compatible with existing gh#-tagged production cards (2026-07-09). Not yet proven against a live GitLab/Gitea VK dispatch end-to-end -- owed to Phase 9's manual verification.<br>[super-fr:test_pr_observe.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_pr_observe.py>) [super-fr:test_bridge_pr_state.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_bridge_pr_state.py>) [super-fr:test_done_reconcile.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_done_reconcile.py>) [super-fr:test_cardref.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_cardref.py>) [super-fr:test_bridge_cli.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_bridge_cli.py>) |
| fr init scaffold on a GitLab- or Gitea-backed repo installs the right CLI into the devcontainer (not github-cli), and fr acceptance init generates a working CI workflow in the right location and schema for that backend | [super-fr:2026-07-09-multi-backend-git-host-adapters-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-multi-backend-git-host-adapters-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_init_scaffold.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | scaffold_profile() picks the host CLI by backend: HOST_CLI_FEATURE for GitHub, and for GitLab/Gitea a postCreate snippet rendered from HOST_CLI_PINS (versioned, per-architecture, sha256-verified glab/tea installs; gh#576 replaced the amd64-only HOST_CLI_POST_CREATE). fr acceptance init picks WORKFLOW_TEMPLATE_GITEA (.gitea/workflows/, same YAML shape as GitHub Actions) or WORKFLOW_TEMPLATE_GITLAB (.gitlab-ci.yml, a genuinely different schema) via fr._hosts.detect_backend. This work also fixed a real blocker it surfaced: resolve_identity()'s remote-parsing regex was GitHub-only, which would have hard-failed fr acceptance init on a fresh GitLab/Gitea repo. Residual documented gap: fr acceptance report --link-mode has no gitea/gitlab mode yet, so source links in the generated report still point at github.com. The CLI install has been proven live in an arm64 container (2026-09-23, glab 1.107.0 / tea 0.14.2, row scaffold-host-cli-arch; the separate pinned-host-cli-assets-resolve row checks the pinned downloads and checksums on a schedule, not a container install). Not yet proven against a live GitLab/Gitea instance; that is owed to the multi-backend spec's Phase 9 manual verification.<br>[super-fr:test_init_scaffold.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_init_scaffold.py>) [super-fr:test_acceptance_init.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_acceptance_init.py>) [super-fr:test_acceptance_cmd.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_acceptance_cmd.py>) |

Live half hand-run 2026-09-19 against a self-hosted GitLab whose default branch is 'master', which is why ref=HEAD rather than a branch name: file_exists True for a present file and False for an absent one, list_dir real entries, read_file real text — all four broken before this PR. 'skipped' because CI cannot reach a private instance.<br>[super-fr:test_real_glabclient.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_real_glabclient.py>) [super-fr:test__urls.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test__urls.py>) |
| An operator whose GitLab is self-hosted reaches it by declaring 'backend: gitlab' alone; 'host:' overrides, and a host declared for a backend fr cannot target says so | [super-fr:2026-09-19-gitlab-contents-ref-and-self-hosted-hosts-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-09-19-gitlab-contents-ref-and-self-hosted-hosts-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test__hosts.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | skipped | All three clauses now hold. (a) 'backend: gitlab' ALONE suffices — verified live from a checkout with no host: key: host_for derived gitlab.internal.example from the git remote and client_for built a RealGlabClient carrying it. (b) 'host:' overrides the derived value. (c) A host declared for a backend fr cannot target warns once, on next use rather than at scaffold time. KNOWN LIMIT, stated in the PR: pr_state's Issue auto-close still targets the SaaS host, blocked on closer's 3-arg arity, not on backend resolution. | Inside a preparer-marked container (mode external), fr isolation up adopts the existing containment instead of isolating a second time, ensures the requested feature branch in place, and the exec-bridge runs commands in the container's own env | [super-fr:2026-07-24-isolation-host-modes-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-07-24-isolation-host-modes-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation_external.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Type 1: preparer writes the marker; up validates+adopts; down never deletes the preparer's checkout. ExternalTarget lifecycle + selection-precedence unit-pinned; live external-container walk = spec Test Plan step 3, post-merge operator-driven.<br>[super-fr:test_isolation_external.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation_external.py>) [super-fr:test_isolation_cmd.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation_cmd.py>) |
| On a host declaring FR_ISOLATION_TARGET=worktree (docker-less pod), fr isolation up/exec/down completes a full workspace lifecycle with no devcontainer or docker invocation and never writes the base clone | [super-fr:2026-07-24-isolation-host-modes-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-07-24-isolation-host-modes-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation_hostworktree.py>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_hostworktree_lifecycle.py>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Type 2: worktree half only; the host env is the env, with no secrets provisioning. The host declaration (FR_ISOLATION_TARGET=worktree) is read at up and gc only. up records the mode in the workspace state, and exec/status/down follow the recorded mode even with the variable unset (gh#569, row isolation-mode-from-state). Lifecycle and the no-docker/devcontainer argv (Runner seam) are pinned by unit and integration tests. Live host walk 2026-09-23 (journal p6-live-569). The live pod walk is spec Test Plan step 2, which also unblocks hermes-agent-compat Phase 8.<br>[super-fr:test_isolation_hostworktree.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation_hostworktree.py>) [super-fr:test_isolation_cmd.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation_cmd.py>) [super-fr:test_hostworktree_lifecycle.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_hostworktree_lifecycle.py>) |

Phase 11 landed the shipped caller: plugins/super-fr/workflows/fr-goal.yaml is now the full spec-§4.A pipeline (not Phase 6's stub), and plugins/super-fr/skills/fr-goal/SKILL.md is rewritten to accept an optional shape argument, drive the resolved shape's cursor via `fr run start/advance/resolve`, and default to `fr-goal` with no argument — the load-bearing back-compat half. test_fr_goal_shape.py proves resolve_workflow finds the real shipped manifest, that its step ids match the skill's numbered narration 1:1 (so the two cannot silently drift), and that a repo-authored docs/superpowers/workflows/fr-goal.yaml overrides it wholesale. Flipped to `ci` rather than the sibling dispatch-granularity rows: this row is specifically about fr-goal/`fr run` selecting a shape, which is now real and CI-covered; it is NOT about fr apply/the bridge deriving dispatch granularity from a resolved shape, which stays hardcoded to fr.workflow.shapes.FR_GOAL_PHASE_DISPATCH (see the sibling rows' notes). Milestone review r2: resolving the shipped shape was covered; EXECUTING it was not, and it could not be executed -- its first step shelled out to `fr isolation up` (moving run state out from under the run) and its second was a gated agent step nothing could clear. test_fr_goal_shape.py now walks the real manifest through the real CLI past both, so "no argument runs today's pipeline" is a checked claim rather than a resolution-only one. Review r5-b5/r5-e14: resolution was covered only where a Claude Code marketplace clone exists. The shipped manifests now also travel INSIDE the fr wheel (packages/fr/src/fr/workflows/, reached via importlib.resources), so lookup is repo override > $FR_SHIPPED_WORKFLOWS_DIR > packaged > marketplace clone, and `fr run start fr-goal` resolves on a hermes pod, an OpenCode consumer, or a bare `uv tool install fr` with an empty HOME. test_tripwire_shipped_workflows.py gained three checks (the packaged set matches the plugin set, is byte-identical, and is reachable through importlib.resources); test_workflow_resolve.py pins the precedence.<br>[super-fr:test_tripwire_shipped_workflows.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_tripwire_shipped_workflows.py>) [super-fr:test_skill_validation.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_skill_validation.py>) [super-fr:test_fr_goal_shape.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_fr_goal_shape.py>) |

## 21 · Run state

| Acceptance | Origin | Levels | Automation | Evidence / notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A run's step cursor and emitted artifacts are durable and reviewable in the PR, so a run can be audited or resumed mid-flight. | [super-fr:2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_state.py>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_fr_goal_shape.py>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Spec 4.B. docs/superpowers/runs/<run-id>.yaml, git-tracked, archives to implemented/runs/ alongside its plan. Durability: byte-stable YAML round-trip (test_run_state.py) plus git mv on archive, mirroring _archive_journal. Resumability is exercised end-to-end, not merely in-memory: every `fr run` CLI test issues separate start/advance/status/resolve/check invocations that each reload state from disk, so a step surviving process exit -- including a gate: operator step left blocked and picked back up later -- is what the tests actually pin. Audit: `fr run status` prints the cursor and every step's state. `fr run advance` never invokes a model (kind: cli executes directly and captures exit+stdout; kind: agent only emits a dispatch brief and marks itself running), asserted by monkeypatching subprocess.run to raise if called on the agent-step path. `fr run resolve` (added in milestone review, spec amended: the original 4-command CLI could mark an agent step running but never move it to done, wedging every real run on its first agent step) records that step's outcome via the same done/failed cursor-advance logic advance's cli branch uses (_complete_step, shared not forked) and is equally non-executing. Archival is keyed on the DATA a run file already carries (the plan step's emitted.plan path), not on any run-id/plan-slug name convention -- a name-keyed version landed first and silently never matched in real use (run ids are date+branch-derived, plan slugs are authored independently), caught by a fixture using a deliberately unrelated run id and plan slug (test_archive_cmd.py); a same-name fixture would have hidden the bug. Phase 7. Milestone review r2 corrected two claims this row's notes had already made but nothing proved. (1) "a gate: operator step ... picked back up later" was false: `advance` marked a gated step blocked and `resolve` refused anything not `running`, so the shipped fr-goal shape -- whose SECOND step is agent+gate -- wedged permanently, verified against the real CLI. The old gate tests asserted only that a step BECOMES blocked, never that it can be cleared; `resolve` now clears one (an agent step completes, a cli step's gate is cleared so the next `advance` still executes it and its exit code is still the verdict), pinned at fixture level in test_run_cli.py AND at shape level in test_fr_goal_shape.py, which walks the SHIPPED manifest start -> blocked brainstorm -> resolve -> the next step's brief. (2) Durability now means durable IN THE WORKSPACE (spec 4.B amended): `fr run start` ensures isolation itself and writes the run file inside the resulting worktree, so `advance` from the worktree finds its own run and cli steps run with cwd at the workspace root -- test_run_workspace.py covers the marker/reuse/refusal paths, and test_run_workspace_lifecycle.py walks a REAL host-worktree isolation (no docker) asserting the base clone comes away with no run file at all. The shipped shape's `isolate` step is gone; isolation is a precondition, not a step the run performs on itself.<br>[super-fr:test_run_state.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_state.py>) [super-fr:test_run_cli.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_cli.py>) [super-fr:test_run_workspace.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_workspace.py>) [super-fr:test_archive_cmd.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_archive_cmd.py>) [super-fr:test_fr_goal_shape.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_fr_goal_shape.py>) [super-fr:test_run_workspace_lifecycle.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_workspace_lifecycle.py>) |
| An operator re-entering the loop cold is told to wait rather than handed a second dispatch brief for an agent that is still running. | [super-fr:2026-09-20-fr-run-cursor-cluster-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-20-fr-run-cursor-cluster-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_cli.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | SUPERSEDED - MERGED INTO run-dispatch-refuses-second (2026-09-21, unit-record-unification spec section 7). One behaviour, one row: witness decision u1 established that "advance refuses a RUNNING unit" and "advance refuses a HELD unit" are the same claim, because the open ATTEMPT is the witness and "running" is lifecycle state everywhere else. Two rows that can only ever move together are one row wearing two labels. KEPT, NOT DELETED, and that is a tooling fact rather than a preference: fr acceptance ships add / set-status / check / report and no delete or edit verb (checked against fr acceptance --help on 2026-09-21), and striking a row out of matrix.yaml by hand is precisely the hand-edit .claude/rules/acceptance-matrix.md forbids. So this row survives as a POINTER. Add no evidence here; read run-dispatch-refuses-second, which now carries this row's test refs as well as its own. Status stays ci and that is not an overclaim: the behaviour named above really is CI-pinned in tests/unit/test_run_cli.py (test_advance_refuses_a_running_member, test_advance_refuses_a_running_top_level_agent_step, test_redispatch_re_emits_the_brief_for_the_outstanding_unit_only, test_redispatch_with_nothing_outstanding_is_refused). A merged row demoted to not-implemented would report a false red for a capability that works. LIVE 2026-09-24, scratch fr-goal run (fr 4.19.2, host-worktree, CLI-level; not mid-way through a model-driven run): a second advance with a unit outstanding exits 2 'ALREADY HELD … (dispatched <ts>)'; --redispatch re-briefs only that unit (cursor diff: old attempt abandoned, new appended); with nothing outstanding --redispatch exits 2 and leaves the cursor byte-identical.<br>[super-fr:test_run_cli.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_cli.py>) |
| A grouped for_each member id copied from advance's output resolves, or the failure teaches the two flags that do. | [super-fr:2026-09-20-fr-run-cursor-cluster-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-20-fr-run-cursor-cluster-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_cli.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Composite member id refused with the two flags spelled out; advance prints the same command before the JSON brief, and the brief stays the last stdout line. LIVE 2026-09-24, scratch fr-goal run (fr 4.19.2, host-worktree, CLI-level; not mid-way through a model-driven run): resolve --step phase/1/implement-phase exits 2 and teaches '--step implement-phase --item phase/1'; the resolve hint precedes the JSON and advance \| tail -1 \| jq parses.<br>[super-fr:test_run_cli.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_cli.py>) |
| A manual phase is never offered to an agent, and the cursor still shows it rather than hiding a deliberate omission. | [super-fr:2026-09-20-fr-run-cursor-cluster-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-20-fr-run-cursor-cluster-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_cli.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | No brief is built for a manual phase on any path; the cursor records phase/<n>: manual so a deliberate omission stays visible; advance and adopt write it identically. The marker keys on tag alone, never on completion, so an already-complete front-loaded manual phase is recorded manual too — the two writers agree without either parsing state. LIVE, Run C (#592, a real Claude Code fr-goal run on #526, 2026-09-23): the trailing [manual] phase 4 got no dispatch unit in the cursor, implement completed, and deliver still opened the PR.<br>[super-fr:test_run_cli.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_cli.py>) [super-fr:test_run_adopt.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_adopt.py>) |

## 22 · Dispatch granularity

| Acceptance | Origin | Levels | Automation | Evidence / notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The granularity dispatch creates work items at (whole run, per plan phase, or per target repo of a spec) is declared by the workflow shape, not hardcoded by the dispatcher. | [super-fr:2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_item_graph.py>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_spec_fanout.py>) ~~UI~~ | not implemented | Spec 4.E. Phase 8 landed the mechanism (fr_dispatch.item_graph.build_items is the single builder and honours all three units) and Phase 9 widened tick's source to Plan \| SpecMeta \| None. Phase 12 removed the hardcoding this note used to name: the bridge no longer rides tick's built-in default but resolves each plan's own shape from _meta.yaml's `workflow:` key (bridge_cli.py -> workflow_for_plan -> tick(workflow=...)), so the dispatcher genuinely reads granularity off the shape. STAYS not-implemented because only one of the three declared units is reachable that way — unit:phase. A unit:run shape needs a run id that plan discovery has no source for, and a unit:spec shape needs a SpecMeta that no shipped caller ever produces (discover_plans yields Plans, fr apply takes a plan dir; SpecMeta appears in no bridge or CLI call site). An operator therefore still cannot SELECT run- or spec-granularity through a shipped path. The remaining gap is pinned rather than described: tests/integration/test_bridge_shape_binding.py::test_a_plan_naming_a_run_unit_shape_is_refused_for_want_of_a_run_id asserts the loud per-plan failure inside the bridge's I9 boundary (daemon survives, nothing dispatched at the wrong granularity). Flip when a shipped caller can dispatch a run- or spec-unit shape end to end.<br>[super-fr:test_item_graph.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_item_graph.py>) [super-fr:test_no_pr_shape.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_no_pr_shape.py>) [super-fr:test_spec_fanout.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_spec_fanout.py>) [super-fr:test_bridge_shape_binding.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_bridge_shape_binding.py>) |
| Dispatching a spec fans out one work item per target repo named in its Implementation Plans table, each carrying a parent reference to the spec. | [super-fr:2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_item_graph.py>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_spec_fanout.py>) ~~UI~~ | not implemented | Spec 4.E/4.F. Phase 9 landed the fan-out end to end at library level — build_items._spec_items decomposes a SpecMeta into one item per distinct target repo (manual/placeholder rows skipped by design, malformed Repo cells reported as failures), tick accepts a SpecMeta source, and tests/integration/test_spec_fanout.py drives a 3-row fixture through tick with a fake runner, each item parented to the spec-level id. Phase 12 added the plan->shape binding this note previously named as the blocker, but that binding does NOT unblock this row: `workflow:` lives in a PLAN's _meta.yaml, while a unit:spec shape decomposes a SpecMeta. No shipped caller ever produces a SpecMeta dispatch source — the bridge's discover_plans yields Plans and fr apply takes a plan dir (grep: SpecMeta appears nowhere in bridge_cli.py or packages/fr/src/fr/commands/). Spec fan-out therefore remains fake-runner-only and operator-unobservable. Flip when a shipped entry point dispatches a spec — a spec target for `fr apply --to`, or discovery that yields specs alongside plans.<br>[super-fr:test_item_graph.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_item_graph.py>) [super-fr:test_spec_fanout.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_spec_fanout.py>) |
| A run-unit goal dispatches with no spec or plan on main (they are its outputs), while a phase-unit dispatch still refuses an unmerged plan. | [super-fr:2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_reachability.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Spec 4.E. Generalizes the 2026-05-17 reachability gate from a hardcoded refusal to a rule derived from each step's declared needs. Phase 8 landed required_inputs (needs minus emits) and made fr apply's gate read it off a manifest; Phase 12 makes that manifest the PLAN's own — fr.commands.apply_cmd._check_plan_reachable_on_origin_head now calls workflow_for_plan(plan, repo_root) instead of the module-level FR_GOAL_PHASE_DISPATCH. Flipped to ci because both halves of the acceptance are now exercised against real git repos through the SHIPPED gate with the shape chosen by the plan rather than by a monkeypatched constant: tests/unit/test_reachability.py::test_a_plan_declaring_a_run_shape_passes_the_gate_with_nothing_on_main (a plan naming a unit:run shape whose spec and plan are its own outputs passes on an unmerged tree) and ::test_a_plan_declaring_no_shape_still_refuses_the_same_unmerged_tree (today's key-less plans resolve FR_GOAL_PHASE_DISPATCH and are still refused). ::test_a_plan_naming_an_unresolvable_shape_raises_rather_than_defaulting and ::test_apply_refuses_a_plan_whose_shape_cannot_be_resolved pin that an unknown shape is a loud refusal (exit 2, zero gh calls), never a silent fall back to the default gate. NOT claimed here: fr apply's own GitHub projection is still per-phase — creating a run-unit WORK ITEM belongs to dispatch-unit-declared-by-shape, which stays not-implemented.<br>[super-fr:test_reachability.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_reachability.py>) [super-fr:test_plan_workflow_binding.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_plan_workflow_binding.py>) |
| After the WorkItem cutover the live VK bridge dispatches an unchanged plan identically: one card per ready phase, no duplicates on a second tick. | [super-fr:2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md>) | ~~UNIT~~ ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_bridge_dispatch_parity.py>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Spec 4.D/6/8 Test Plan step 3. Executable half: test_bridge_dispatch_parity.py (one card per ready phase, zero new cards on a second tick), in CI. Live half walked 2026-09-07 against the real board with the one-phase fixture plan 2026-09-05-dispatch-walk-toy (super-fr#447): the 06:56 UTC bridge tick (fr 4.0.0) created exactly one VK workspace (FFE-249 -> gh#447) and stamped fr:synced; the 07:04 tick found 0 discoverable plans and created nothing; the 7 pre-cutover cards were fetched for dedup and none duplicated. Two operational faults found on the way were not dispatch regressions: vk-local logged out since 2026-08-01 (relay/host offline, remote calls 401) and a slot budget of 0 from 8 non-archived finished workspaces.<br>[super-fr:test_bridge_dispatch_parity.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_bridge_dispatch_parity.py>) |
| A plan naming a shape that does not resolve fails that plan loudly rather than silently falling back to the default shape, and never takes the bridge daemon down. | [super-fr:2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_plan_workflow_binding.py>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_bridge_shape_binding.py>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Spec 4.A.1 and Test Plan 8.2 step 10. A silent fallback would dispatch a plan at the WRONG granularity while reporting success - the failure mode this design produced most often. Resolution sits inside the bridge's existing per-plan I9 boundary, so a bad shape fails that plan and the tick continues.<br>[super-fr:test_plan_workflow_binding.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_plan_workflow_binding.py>) [super-fr:test_bridge_shape_binding.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_bridge_shape_binding.py>) |

## 23 · Runner and tracker negotiation

| Acceptance | Origin | Levels | Automation | Evidence / notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A workflow shape whose required capabilities a runner cannot provide is refused at preflight with one clear message, instead of failing part-way through a run. | [super-fr:2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_capabilities.py>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_bridge_shape_binding.py>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Spec 4.F. Phase 5 landed the mechanism — tick.required_capabilities is checked against Runner.capabilities BEFORE runner.preflight runs, reusing the preflight blocker's per-item failure path (one message, synced=0, dispatch never called) — but nothing shipped resolved a shape and fed `requires` into it, so it was pinned unit-only. Phase 12 supplies that caller: packages/fr-vk/src/fr_vk/bridge_cli.py resolves each plan's own shape (fr.workflow.resolve.workflow_for_plan, off _meta.yaml's optional `workflow:` key) and passes required_capabilities=frozenset(manifest.requires) into tick. Flipped to ci on tests/integration/test_bridge_shape_binding.py::test_a_runner_lacking_the_shapes_capability_refuses_the_dispatch, which drives a whole bridge_cli.main() tick with the REAL tick (not a spy): the plan's shape requires [git, browser], the runner provides {git, scm}, and the tick reports synced=0 errors=1 skipped=1 with runner.dispatched == [] and runner.preflight never called — the capability short-circuit specifically, not a preflight or dispatch failure. Fixture discipline: that shape's `requires` deliberately differ from FR_GOAL_PHASE_DISPATCH's {git, tests, scm}, so the refusal can only come from the plan's own declaration being read.<br>[super-fr:test_capabilities.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_capabilities.py>) [super-fr:test_bridge_shape_binding.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_bridge_shape_binding.py>) |
| A tracker instance declares which item states it can express, per project; a shape needing an unsupported state is refused up front rather than erroring against the tracker mid-run. | [super-fr:2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-14-workflow-shapes-and-workitem-dispatch-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_tracker.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Spec 4.G, Phase 10. Flipped not-implemented -> ci deliberately, not just because a test file exists -- the PROTOCOL-LEVEL claim in `acceptance` above is now genuinely exercised, not merely modeled. fr.tracker.model.state_supported and GithubTracker.supports make "an instance declares which states it can express" real (null or absent means unsupported, per-instance mapping resolved repo-over-user like fr.models); fr_dispatch._tracker_blocker makes "refused up front" real -- chained into the SAME preflight short-circuit Phase 5 built for capability negotiation (§4.F), before runner.preflight ever runs, never mid-tick. Two gaps this row does NOT cover, flagged rather than silently claimed -- no Jira adapter is built (GithubTracker is the one concrete Tracker and supports all five states by default; the partial-mapping case is exercised via a fake tracker plus a GithubTracker instance override, not a second real backend), and spec 4.G's "validate against the live server's actually-available transitions" half is unimplemented -- fr.tracker.model.check_tracker_mapping_shape validates config SHAPE only (an unresolvable instance, an unknown state key) and its own docstring says so.<br>[super-fr:test_tracker.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_tracker.py>) |
| fr apply --to <runner> refuses, before any forge call, a runner that takes no phase work; and a tick that refuses an item for its unit says so instead of blaming the repo. | [super-fr:2026-09-27-apply-runner-units.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/journals/debug/2026-09-27-apply-runner-units.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_apply_runner_units.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | super-fr#644. Runner.units is a class attribute read off the registered entry point (fr_dispatch.registry.runner_units) without building the runner, since vk and cncd have no from_env. apply refuses a runner whose units lack phase (herdr: run only) or that declares none, with exit 2 and _make_gh_client never called. vk, cncd and herdr each gate can_dispatch on their own units, so the declaration and the per-item gate cannot drift. tick reports a unit refusal as unit_mismatch naming the units the runner does take; any other can_dispatch refusal is reported as a refusal naming the runner, unit and repo, with the historical unknown_repo metric reason kept.<br>[super-fr:test_apply_runner_units.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_apply_runner_units.py>) |

The tripwire pins that one import; nothing yet asserts the installed CLI actually runs. Test Plan 8.0 covers it manually until smoke-suite-automated lands.<br>[super-fr:test_tripwire_no_direct_click_import.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_tripwire_no_direct_click_import.py>) |

Not built in this PR - operator-requested follow-up. Each step is currently manual; the class of defect it guards (a release whose CLI cannot start) is invisible to the in-workspace suite. Review r5-b5 sharpened WHY this row matters: step 8.0.3 (`fr workflow check --all`) used to print "no workflow shapes found" and exit 0, so the manual smoke suite REPORTED SUCCESS on a host where no shape was installed at all — the exact state that step exists to detect. `--all` now exits 1 on zero shapes (fr.commands.workflow_cmd, tests/unit/test_workflow_check.py), so automating this row would now catch the defect rather than inherit a green false negative. Until it is automated the whole suite still depends on an operator running it. |

| A session holding an fr pipeline in repo A can start isolation in repo B — 'cd <repo-B> && fr isolation up' is allowed, not denied by the guard that recommends it | [super-fr:2026-07-26-fr-goal-isolation-deadlocks-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-07-26-fr-goal-isolation-deadlocks-design.md#D>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_hooks_guard.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | super-fr#421. TestCrossRepoReachability -- cd <repo-B> && fr isolation up/status/init is allowed so another repo's isolation is reachable, and once entered its worktree (valid marker) is a legitimate destination. The fixture carries a live linked worktree so the #341 self-heal cannot mask the result, fenced by test_precondition_base_repo_still_denied.<br>[super-fr:test_hooks_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_hooks_guard.py>) |
| Making a second repo reachable does not weaken the guard in its own repo: base-repo-cwd commands, and a cd back into the base repo, stay denied | [super-fr:2026-07-26-fr-goal-isolation-deadlocks-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-07-26-fr-goal-isolation-deadlocks-design.md#D>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_hooks_guard.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | super-fr#421 regression fence, all three directions. Repo A: base cwd denied, a cd back into it denied, a cd into a non-repo denied. Another repo (TestOtherRepoStillHonoursItsOwnIsolation): reaching it does NOT license general work there -- mutations in an un-isolated clone are denied with a reason naming that repo, and its fr isolation down cannot clear this session's sentinel. Sensitive paths (TestSensitivePathsStayOutOfReach): cd ~/.ssh is denied whether or not $HOME is a dotfiles git repo -- the allowance is keyed on being an isolation workspace, not on repo identity, precisely so a dotfiles $HOME cannot expose a private key. All 31 pre-existing guard tests preserved.<br>[super-fr:test_hooks_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_hooks_guard.py>) |

## 27 · fr-isolation pipeline guard

| Acceptance | Origin | Levels | Automation | Evidence / notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| An allowed `fr …` command reached through a leading `cd` cannot launder a rider into a path that is denied without it; the strip only folds in a `cd` that lands inside the pipeline's repo or on another repo's toplevel, and only the first line of a command is ever evaluated. | [super-fr:2026-07-26-fr-goal-isolation-deadlocks-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-07-26-fr-goal-isolation-deadlocks-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_hooks_guard.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | TestAllowedFrCommandDoesNotCarryARider: the ~/.ssh rider in both fr isolation and fr --version forms, the multi-line smuggling form, plus regression fences that #421's own reach (cd <repo-B> && fr isolation up) and journal p3-f1 (same-repo subdir) still pass. The no-cd rider is a recorded, deliberate boundary (test_preexisting_rider_without_a_cd_is_still_allowed).<br>[super-fr:test_hooks_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_hooks_guard.py>) |

## 29 · isolation-traceability

| Acceptance | Origin | Levels | Automation | Evidence / notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| An operator can ask fr which workspace an agent session is attached to, and which sessions hold a workspace, without inspecting the harness. | [super-fr:2026-09-04-worktree-traceability-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-04-worktree-traceability-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation_sessions.py>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_hooks_session_bind.py>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Unit level (phase 1): fr.isolation.sessions attach/detach/detach_all/stale index classification, IsolationState.sessions round-trip, and the CLI surface (attach, detach, status --session / sessions column, up --session, down unbind + warning) pinned per-PR. Hook level (phase 3, filed under int because the matrix has no hook level): fr-session-bind.sh (PostToolUse Bash -> attach on up/exec with and without a leading cd, detach on down, subagent and non-Bash input ignored) and fr-session-unbind.sh (SessionEnd -> detach, resume kept) exercised via subprocess against a stub fr that records argv. The unbind additionally pins the SessionEnd time budget: against a stub fr that blocks 3s, the hook must return inside 1.5s (Claude Code aborts a SessionEnd hook at SESSION_END_HOOK_TIMEOUT_MS_DEFAULT and reports "Hook cancelled"; a plugin cannot raise that floor) and the detach must still land afterwards, so returning early never degrades into dropping the unbind and leaking the binding. | The Claude Code status line shows the branch this session works on and its fr-isolation worktree path (or no fr-isolation), green when fr and purple when not, well inside one status-line refresh (0.5 s CI guard). | [super-fr:2026-09-04-worktree-traceability-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-04-worktree-traceability-design.md>) [super-fr:2026-09-14-statusline-session-branch-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-09-14-statusline-session-branch-design.md>) | ~~UNIT~~ ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_statusline_segment.py>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Contract v2 (spec 2026-09-14). Golden subprocess tests, filed under int because the matrix has no golden level. Segment plugins/super-fr/scripts/fr-statusline-segment.sh (test_statusline_segment.py): a live binding wins, a stale binding (worktree gone) is ignored, a cwd inside an fr workspace is fr, plain linked and detached agent worktrees are none, non-repo and missing cwd, the plain / ansi / oneline formats and --cwd, a 0.5 s CI timing guard, and a trap fr on PATH proving the fr CLI is never invoked. Reference status line plugins/super-fr/scripts/fr-statusline-claude.sh (test_statusline_claude.py): three lines (model, ctx and rates; branch row and ~/cwd; worktree row), a symlinked script still finds the segment, a missing segment degrades to purple none rows, and float percentages are floored. Measured on the operator Mac (2026-09-14, /bin/bash 3.2, Xcode git first on PATH): bound about 77 ms, unbound in a repo about 100 ms, non-repo about 65 ms. Operator-side wiring (~/.claude/statusline.sh) is the manual phase 5. LIVE 2026-09-24 (P7): with ~/.claude/statusline.sh symlinked to the cache's fr-statusline-claude.sh, the demo2 session showed 'branch: wt/demo2 \| ~/.cache/fr/worktrees/super-fr/wt__demo2' / 'worktree: <that path>', and a native non-fr worktree showed 'no fr-isolation'.<br>[super-fr:test_statusline_segment.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_statusline_segment.py>) [super-fr:test_statusline_claude.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_statusline_claude.py>) |

## 30 · isolation-conformity

| Acceptance | Origin | Levels | Automation | Evidence / notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| claude --worktree <name> and EnterWorktree in an fr-enabled repo create an fr workspace at the fr location with the session bound; subagent worktrees keep the Claude default shape. | [super-fr:2026-09-04-worktree-traceability-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-04-worktree-traceability-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation_sessions_cmd.py>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_hooks_worktree.py>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Unit level (phase 4): fr isolation up --print-path (last non-empty stdout line is the worktree path, human lines on stderr, idempotent for an existing branch+worktree) and fr isolation down --worktree <path> (resolves the workspace by worktree path from any cwd; unknown path exits 2). Hook level (filed under int because the matrix has no hook level): fr-worktree-create.sh (WorktreeCreate -> fr isolation up --repo --branch wt/<name>\|<name-with-slash> --session --harness claude --print-path in an fr-enabled repo, FR_ISOLATION_TARGET=worktree exported when no devcontainer profile; agent-* names and non-fr repos reproduce the Claude default <repo>/.claude/worktrees/<name> detached worktree; non-git cwd exits 1) and fr-worktree-remove.sh (WorktreeRemove -> git worktree remove for agent-*, else fr isolation down --worktree, refusal reported on stderr with exit 0) exercised via subprocess against a stub fr that records argv. LIVE 2026-09-24 (worktree-traceability P7.T2.S2, Claude Code 2.1.281, fr 4.19.2). On a DEFAULT install it FAILED: the plugin-registered WorktreeCreate is never invoked for `claude --worktree`, and the session landed in <repo>/.claude/worktrees/demo. With the same hooks also registered in ~/.claude/settings.json it PASSED: `claude --worktree demo2` landed in ~/.cache/fr/worktrees/super-fr/wt__demo2, bound its session, and `exit` removed the workspace and container via WorktreeRemove. install.sh now registers them there (tests/integration/test_install_worktree_settings_hooks.py), and the create hook is serialized against a double registration. Startup waits for a full devcontainer up. EnterWorktree was not walked live.<br>[super-fr:test_isolation_sessions_cmd.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation_sessions_cmd.py>) [super-fr:test_hooks_worktree.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_hooks_worktree.py>) [super-fr:test_install_worktree_settings_hooks.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_install_worktree_settings_hooks.py>) |
| fr isolation up run from inside any worktree of a repo files the workspace under the main checkout name. | [super-fr:2026-09-04-worktree-traceability-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-04-worktree-traceability-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation_repo_key.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Unit level (phase 2): repo_cache_name resolves the main checkout basename for the main checkout, a native agent worktree (<repo>/.claude/worktrees/agent-*) and a nested worktree elsewhere, falling back to the basename outside git; a host-mode `up` launched from inside the agent worktree lands under ~/.cache/fr/worktrees/<main-checkout>/<branch>, never under agent-*. Pinned per-PR.<br>[super-fr:test_isolation_repo_key.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation_repo_key.py>) |
| In an fr-enabled repo, a request that would invoke using-git-worktrees is routed to fr-isolation; no .worktrees directory is created. | [super-fr:2026-09-04-worktree-traceability-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-04-worktree-traceability-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_tripwire_worktrees_dir.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Rule + tripwire (phase 6): the routing claim itself lives in the rule text plugins/super-fr/rules/fr-worktree-override.md (installed to ~/.claude/rules by install.sh, mirrored to .opencode/instructions and the Hermes SOUL block), and a rule is prose the harness follows, not code a test can execute. The unit tripwire pins what CAN be checked: the rule exists and carries the claim (using-git-worktrees, fr isolation up --branch, never create .worktrees/), git ls-files has no path with a .worktrees or .claude/worktrees component, and .gitignore lists both. Pinned per-PR.<br>[super-fr:test_tripwire_worktrees_dir.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_tripwire_worktrees_dir.py>) |

## 32 · Artifact migration

| Acceptance | Origin | Levels | Automation | Evidence / notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| An operator whose fr version changed under in-flight work has their artifacts migrated by the very next fr command, which then completes normally. | [super-fr:2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_migration_trigger.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Spec 3.C. The 'pause, migrate, resume' the operator asked for, where the pause is invisible because the typed command still runs. Unit-pinned end to end through the real typer app - a stale plan, an interactive context, `fr skills` still prints - plus the non-interactive refusal that must never migrate or commit. Test Plan item 1 (a live node upgrade) stays operator-driven.<br>[super-fr:test_migration_trigger.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_migration_trigger.py>) |
| The migration commit contains only the artifact paths it rewrote; unrelated uncommitted work is left in the working tree, unmodified and uncommitted. | [super-fr:2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_migration_commit.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Spec 3.D. In-flight work IS a dirty tree, so refusing until clean would block the case this exists for; stash cycles risk a pop conflict over unsaved work. Every fixture therefore starts dirty and the load-bearing assertions are negative - an unrelated MODIFIED file stays modified and uncommitted (kills `git add -A`), an unrelated STAGED file is not swept in (kills a plain `git commit -m`, which records the whole index). Both mutations were run and both fail the suite.<br>[super-fr:test_migration_commit.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_migration_commit.py>) |
| The bridge and CI refuse loudly and exit non-zero on stale artifacts rather than skipping them silently, and never auto-commit. | [super-fr:2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_vk_bridge_discover.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Spec 3.C. discover_plans (fr_dispatch/__init__.py) no longer catches PlanSchemaError as a quiet warning-and-continue; it logs ERROR, pushes a `stale_artifact` failure metric, and reports the failure to bridge_cli.py via a `failures` outparam that folds into the tick error total -- one stale plan excludes only itself (I9 boundary), the daemon never migrates or commits (the checkout is hard-reset every tick, issue #286). The "and CI" half was already covered by Phase 3/4's `ensure_artifacts_current` CLI-entry gate (non-interactive when `CI` is set), unchanged here.<br>[super-fr:test_vk_bridge_discover.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_vk_bridge_discover.py>) |
| A plan already under way acquires a run cursor reflecting the work actually done, instead of being stranded without one. | [super-fr:2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_adopt.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Spec 3.E. `fr run adopt <plan-dir\|spec>` reconstructs a cursor from artifacts that already exist, one row of the inference table per test: spec only -> `plan`; plan with no phase complete -> `implement`; some phases complete -> `implement` with per-phase state in the fan-out step's `items`; all complete, no PR -> `review`; an OBSERVED-open PR -> `deliver`. Adoption records `emitted.spec`/`emitted.plan`, so archival-by-emitted.plan still keys correctly — pinned end-to-end by archiving the plan and asserting the run follows it into implemented/runs/, with a plan slug that deliberately does not resemble the branch-derived run id. The other direction is pinned too: a plan whose work is complete is never offered a cursor, and the CLI-entry migration gate REPORTS the offer without ever creating a run (only `fr run adopt` and `fr migrate artifacts --yes --adopt` write one).<br>[super-fr:test_run_adopt.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_adopt.py>) [super-fr:test_migration_trigger.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_migration_trigger.py>) |
| Every artifact fr generates declares the version it was written for, and a file with no stamp reads as version 1 rather than as an error. | [super-fr:2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_artifact_registry.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | not implemented | Spec 3.A. Per-artifact stamps chosen over a repo manifest because a manifest cannot describe a half-migrated tree. Phase 1 lands the registry and a reader/writer per kind, and the unit refs cover the second half of the claim in CI — an absent stamp reads as version 1, not an error, for all five kinds, and `fr validate artifacts` now fails an unreadable or newer-than-this-fr stamp on every kind. RE-EVALUATED IN PHASE 7 AND DELIBERATELY LEFT RED. The FIRST half is still unmet and nothing in this plan changed it — only plans carry a stamp in practice, while `fr journal add`, `fr run start` / `fr run adopt` (save_run_state) and the spec writer all emit unstamped files. Absence READING as 1 is not the same claim as every generated artifact DECLARING its version, and flipping this row on that equivalence would be exactly the overclaim the row exists to prevent. What closes it — the generators write the stamp, which is owed at the moment a kind's current_version first moves past 1, in the same PR that must widen that kind's extra=forbid model with an optional defaulted schema_version field (finding f-closed-world-models-reject-a-stamp; obligation now standing in .claude/rules/artifact-versioning.md).<br>[super-fr:test_artifact_registry.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_artifact_registry.py>) [super-fr:test_validate_artifacts.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_validate_artifacts.py>) |
| Re-running any migration produces no second rewrite and no second commit. | [super-fr:2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_migration_runner.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Spec 3.B. The no-second-REWRITE half is ci-pinned both ways — the stamp guards a schema migration, the predicate guards a repair, and both assert bytes AND mtime unchanged on the second run. The no-second-COMMIT half landed with Phase 4 and is now pinned too, which is what moves this row to ci — test_a_report_whose_paths_are_already_committed_makes_no_empty_commit (re-committing the same report leaves HEAD where it was) and, one level up, test_a_current_tree_neither_migrates_nor_commits (a tree already current never reaches the runner or the committer at all). Test Plan item 7 — the same two commands on a live node — stays operator-driven.<br>[super-fr:test_migration_runner.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_migration_runner.py>) [super-fr:test_migration_fr_version.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_migration_fr_version.py>) [super-fr:test_migration_commit.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_migration_commit.py>) [super-fr:test_migration_trigger.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_migration_trigger.py>) |
| Every version ships a structural validator for every artifact kind it generates, and CI runs it over the repo's own artifacts. | [super-fr:2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_validate_artifacts.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Spec 3.F. `fr validate artifacts [--kind K]` checks the stamp (unreadable, stale, and newer-than-this-fr each fail, the last with an upgrade message — spec 2 forbids downgrades) and then the shape, through `ArtifactKind.validate`. "Every KIND" is structural, not vigilance: `validate` is a required field on the frozen dataclass, so a kind cannot be registered without a validator, and the tripwire keeps the kind list in one module. Both halves of "CI runs it": a `validate-artifacts` job in ci.yml, and test_this_repos_own_artifacts_are_structurally_valid, which fails locally before CI does. The spec-structure check is the one that earns the row — it reproduces commit 7ece5a9's splice (index() matched an INLINE mention of `## Implementation Plans` before the replaced section, so end < start and the tail was re-appended) and fires on both signals; run against the real damaged blob it reports 15 problems, and 0 against every spec in the tree today.<br>[super-fr:test_validate_artifacts.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_validate_artifacts.py>) [super-fr:test_tripwire_artifact_kinds.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_tripwire_artifact_kinds.py>) |
| After a major fr bump, archived plans still report their real state in spec status instead of reading as missing, while execution paths still refuse an incompatible live plan. | [super-fr:2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-08-30-artifact-migration-framework-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_v2_parse.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Spec 3.E.1. Excluding archives from migration collides with the parse-time gate: spec.py:370 catches PlanSchemaError and degrades the plan to Missing, which under 4.0.0 hits all 38 archived plans here. The gate is over-scoped, not the archives - it exists to stop an incompatible fr EXECUTING a plan, and reading history executes nothing. fr.parser.parse gained enforce_fr_version (keyword-only, default True); fr.spec.compute_status is the ONE call site passing False. Every execution path keeps the enforcing default and is pinned by its own test: fr pickup (test_pickup_still_enforces_fr_version), fr status / fr apply's shared build_plan_report read path (test_build_plan_report_still_enforces_fr_version), and the bridge's discover_plans (test_discover_plans_still_enforces_fr_version), each using a fixture whose fr_version ceiling genuinely excludes a monkeypatched installed version.<br>[super-fr:test_v2_parse.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_v2_parse.py>) [super-fr:test_v2_spec.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_v2_spec.py>) [super-fr:test_v2_pickup.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_v2_pickup.py>) [super-fr:test_status_cmd.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_status_cmd.py>) [super-fr:test_vk_bridge_discover.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_vk_bridge_discover.py>) |

The partial case is induced by deleting one line from a real measured capture, because no real cursor carries one. The brainstorm's smaller 43 plans / 1444 steps figure was the same two roots read with enforce_fr_version=True — the delta is the VERSION GATE, not root coverage (38 archived plans carry an fr_version ceiling a 4.x fr can never satisfy; the live root alone is only 5 plans / 134 steps). Stated because an earlier version of this note said 'active-roots count', which would send a future maintainer to widen the wrong thing. #428's literal pattern list scored 237 false positives on bare 'dispatch' alone, 16.7% of steps — super-fr is a repo about dispatch. Any future widening that would break super-fr's own plans now fails here, in CI, instead of in an operator's run.<br>[super-fr:test_dispatch_lint_corpus.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_dispatch_lint_corpus.py>) |

Pinned at both levels: the predicate says held (unit), and the hook is silent against the real fr with a unit dispatched (int). A second, harness-side witness was found on the 2.1.278 binary and is honoured too: a non-empty background_tasks or session_crons in the Stop payload means the session is paused and will be woken, so the guard does not act. That field's POPULATED shape was read from the binary's schema, not captured — stated in tests/fixtures/hooks/NOTE.md. gh#531: line anchors re-pointed to the tests they were recorded for (they had slid onto other tests as lines moved).<br>[super-fr:test_run_idle.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_idle.py#test_stop_3_a_held_unit_is_not_idle>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#test_silent_while_a_dispatched_executor_is_still_working>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#test_in_flight_background_work_means_the_session_will_be_woken>) |
| On OpenCode, an idle run is continued by re-injecting the next command when the session goes idle | [super-fr:2026-09-20-unit-record-unification-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-09-20-unit-record-unification-design.md>) | ~~UNIT~~ ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | not implemented | LIVE-ONLY BY NATURE - stays not-implemented, deliberately. The handler exists (packages/fr-opencode-plugin/src/idle.ts, wired as the existing plugin's event hook) and bun test covers it - 50 tests, 10 mutants killed: exactly one promptAsync per run position on session.idle, silent otherwise, child sessions left alone, never throws. But every one of those tests drives a FAKE client. That a plugin-originated prompt on session.idle actually EXECUTES in a live OpenCode session - that the event fires for a TUI session at the moment a turn ends, that promptAsync is callable from inside an event hook, that the injected text is acted on, and that it does not collide with the operator typing - is shown by none of them, and that is the claim this row makes. By the gh#494 standard it needs a live run; until then parity.yaml says opencode: partial with a scope_note saying why. The adapter cannot BLOCK a stop, only continue the session - a weaker tier than the Claude Code guard, declared as such rather than implied to be parity. VERSION CORRECTION (phase 6, carried into prose and parity.yaml in phase 7): the event and the endpoint were verified in @opencode-ai/sdk TYPE DEFINITIONS on the authoring machine, of versions 1.17.15, 1.1.27 and 1.0.23 - all three carrying EventSessionIdle {properties.sessionID}, Session.parentID, and session.promptAsync -> /session/{id}/prompt_async with body.parts and query.directory. 1.18.31 is the opencode BINARY's version and is NOT an SDK copy on disk here; the earlier claim "verified in the installed 1.18.31 SDK types" (spec section 4.G table, parity.yaml scope_note) was wrong about WHICH artifact was read and both are now corrected. The design is unaffected - every version found carries both - but a verification is only worth its provenance. |
| The idle guard acts at most once per cursor position, so a failing advance cannot trap a session in a loop | [super-fr:2026-09-20-unit-record-unification-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-09-20-unit-record-unification-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_idle.py#test_the_position_moves_when_the_cursor_does_and_only_then>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#test_fails_open_when_it_cannot_remember_having_acted>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | The loop breaker. fr emits an opaque position (run, cursor, step state, gate, each unit's state and attempt count — no timestamp, so a failing advance that only re-stamps a record is not progress). The hook remembers the position it last acted on beside the session binding, WRITES AND READS IT BACK before blocking, and does not block at all if it cannot: a second stop at the same position passes, and it acts again once the cursor moves. Independent brakes: stop_hook_active must be literally false (the CAPTURED continuation payload is let through with no memory at all; a build that drops the field silences the guard rather than un-braking it), and Claude Code's own CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP (default 8, read from the binary). fr isolation detach deletes the memory with the binding. gh#531: line anchors re-pointed to the tests they were recorded for (they had slid onto other tests as lines moved).<br>[super-fr:test_run_idle.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_idle.py#test_the_position_moves_when_the_cursor_does_and_only_then>) [super-fr:test_run_idle.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_idle.py#test_a_failing_advance_does_not_move_the_position>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#test_fails_open_when_it_cannot_remember_having_acted>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#test_it_acts_at_most_once_per_cursor_position>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#test_it_acts_again_once_the_cursor_has_moved>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#test_stop_hook_active_is_an_independent_brake>) |
| A harness-specific argument named outside a scoped per-harness clause, in any skill, agent or rule (canonical or mirror) and in any spelling, fails CI | [super-fr:2026-09-22-harness-argument-neutrality-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-22-harness-argument-neutrality-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_tripwire_skill_tool_neutrality.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Spec 3.A-C, phase 3. ARGUMENT_VOCABULARY (isolation flag in every spelling, run_in_background, Hermes background=true) is scanned by scan_prose everywhere; the tripwire covers skills, agents and rules (canonical plugins/super-fr/rules + .claude/rules, mirrors .opencode/instructions + .hermes/SOUL.d) and asserts zero violations. Closes the gap the adversarial review of #532 found: the flag was scanned only in agents, in one spelling; run_in_background nowhere; rules not at all.<br>[super-fr:test_tripwire_skill_tool_neutrality.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_tripwire_skill_tool_neutrality.py>) [super-fr:test_harness_vocabulary.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_harness_vocabulary.py>) |
