A real headless /fr-goal on OpenCode (github-copilot/gpt-5.6-terra) dispatched a phase: opencode.db child session ses_f3b00175… with parent_id set, agent=fr-phase-executor-mechanical (the tier the plan phase declared), on the answered model, with its own usage and its 32 tool calls recorded under the child rather than the orchestrator. Evidence: the gh#494 phase-6 and gh#498 phase-4 journals (p6-live-evidence, p4-live-evidence). STILL skipped, correctly: reproducing it needs a live opencode binary and a model, neither available in CI. SCOPE, unchanged from before and still true: the headline proves a genuine dispatch, not that tiering resolved — the tiering claim lives in opencode-tier-binding-reaches-dispatch. The headline cannot be corrected because there is no fr acceptance edit verb; that is #522, since folded into #510. CORRECTION: an earlier version of this note said a follow-up for that verb had been filed. It had not — #522 found the claim false. #522 became that follow-up and is now folded into #510. Found by the same run, and filed: #537 (fr's cursor names the wrong holder for an OpenCode dispatch, and cannot name the tier agent that ran) and #538 (fr-goal never asked the model-per-tier question it is gated on). DISCRIMINATING RUN, 2026-09-22 (the model-level proof the earlier runs could not give): the 2026-09-21 run's orchestrator and child both ran on gpt-5.6-terra and all three tiers were bound to it, so its session-row model could not tell a tiered dispatch from an untiered one; its proof rested on the agent NAME alone, for one tier. This run bound FOUR distinct models - mechanical github-copilot/gpt-5.6-luna, standard github-copilot/gpt-5.6-terra, hard github-copilot/gpt-5.6-sol, orchestrator github-copilot/claude-sonnet-5 - in a throwaway local repo (fr-tier-proof, local bare origin, no forge resource) whose plan declared phases 1/2/3 as mechanical/standard/hard. opencode.db, root ses_f37014a55ffePrm0GvcXNde25v (build, claude-sonnet-5), three parented children in phase order: ses_f36fd32e5ffeLnEhUiVhc8dhwj fr-phase-executor-mechanical gpt-5.6-luna $0.0132; ses_f36fb6911ffeFuz6aUuFfsbWjz fr-phase-executor-standard gpt-5.6-terra $0.1326; ses_f36f9f94dffeXlvlEQ6t1mCyBs fr-phase-executor-hard gpt-5.6-sol $0.3867 (run total $1.71). All six assertions PASS: three children; declared tier -> tier agent in order; each child on its tier's binding (read from the installed agent files, last written before the run and stable through it); NO child on the session model; child models pairwise distinct; bindings themselves distinct, i.e. the test could have failed. That closes the gap the scope note above records: tiering resolves to the bound MODEL per tier, not merely to a tier-named agent. #537 reproduced in the same run: fr's cursor records agent_type super-fr:fr-phase-executor for all three, never the tier agent that ran - though it does record the three child session ids, matching opencode.db exactly, so the two stores can be joined by session id. Still skipped: live-only, no CI level. PAID RUN (2026-09-23, #564 Test Plan step 3, fr 4.17.2, real feature #457 -> PR #586, orchestrator build on github-copilot/gpt-6-luna). Cursor vs opencode.db: both phases (plan tier mechanical) were dispatched to fr-phase-executor-mechanical on github-copilot/claude-haiku-4.5 = the binding; each cursor holder id equals its child session id exactly; both review-phase reviewer= ids equal the dispatched general child sessions exactly (on gpt-6-luna); every child is parented to the root; deliver carries tests=<log>. The cursor's agent_type still reads super-fr:fr-phase-executor, never the tier agent (#537). The question batch WAS asked and the turn ended; the operator answered. But brainstorm was resolved with no --emitted spec= and no --answered-by operator, so the cursor records answered_by=agent and no spec, and fr accepted it (#587). Row stays skipped (live-only). |

Mutation-verified, and verified live against this feature's own run cursor writing nothing (journal x-refusal-live, x-refusal-verified-independently). Test Plan item 15 repeats it live post-merge. Phase 4 of the unit-record plan added the cross-session arm: the refusal says the holder was dispatched from ANOTHER session and that its cost is not observable from here (test_advance_refusing_another_sessions_holder_says_it_cannot_see_it), while the ordinary refusal deliberately acquires no such sentence (test_advance_refusing_its_own_sessions_holder_says_nothing_about_sessions). MERGE (2026-09-21, unit-record-unification spec section 7): gh#519's row run-advance-refuses-running is ABSORBED HERE - one behaviour, one row. Witness decision u1 (spec journal) made them literally the same claim: the witness IS the open attempt, and "the unit is running" is lifecycle state everywhere else, so the two rows could only ever move together and a reader had no way to tell which one to trust. THIS row is canonical. The other is NOT deleted - the matrix ships add / set-status / check and no delete or edit verb (checked: fr acceptance --help), and removing a row by hand is exactly the hand-edit the acceptance-matrix rule forbids - so it remains as a pointer back here. Its evidence is folded in: the grouped-member and flat-step arms (test_advance_refuses_a_running_member, test_advance_refuses_a_running_top_level_agent_step, test_a_running_member_with_no_dispatch_record_is_still_refused, test_a_running_flat_step_with_no_dispatch_record_is_still_refused, test_a_flat_agent_step_is_refused_the_same_way) and the --redispatch escape (test_redispatch_re_emits_the_brief_for_the_outstanding_unit_only, test_redispatch_with_nothing_outstanding_is_refused, test_redispatch_is_the_way_out_of_a_recordless_running_unit).<br>[super-fr:test_run_cli.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_cli.py>) |

The mechanism's unit half landed in phase 4 and is genuinely covered: the refusal now says the holder was dispatched from ANOTHER session and that its cost is not observable from here, and names fr run claim --abandoned (tests/unit/test_run_cli.py::test_advance_refusing_another_sessions_holder_says_it_cannot_see_it, with test_advance_refusing_its_own_sessions_holder_says_nothing_about_sessions pinning that the ORDINARY refusal does not acquire that sentence), and the window is refused for an attempt this session did not dispatch (tests/unit/test_run_telemetry.py::test_the_window_is_refused_for_an_attempt_this_session_did_not_dispatch). But this row's claim is TWO REAL HOSTS: a cursor pushed mid-phase and pulled on another machine whose transcripts (~/.claude/projects) and session bindings (~/.cache/fr/sessions) never travelled with the branch, and whose executor may have died with its host. No unit test can be two machines - every such test simulates absence by not writing a file, which is the thing being tested. Flipping it on the unit evidence would report a live claim as verified. What closes it: a live demonstration, Test Plan item 16's sibling. Phase 7 shipped the operator-facing half in fr-goal SKILL.md section 5 ("Picking the run up somewhere else?"), so the recovery is now documented where an operator meets it - documentation is not verification, and the row stays red until the walk happens. |

Non-vacuity: the same call with same_session=True DOES return the stranger. test_an_attempt_with_no_recorded_session_never_borrows_the_window and test_a_process_with_no_session_of_its_own_measures_nothing_by_window cover the two not-knowing cases; test_status_says_a_cost_from_another_session_is_not_observable_from_here covers the rendering. | Each dispatched batch bumps to a version reserved in merge order, so a later batch never ships a lower version and its version conflict after an earlier merge is resolved without the operator | [super-fr:2026-09-25-triage-batches-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-25-triage-batches-design.md#3d-version-reservations>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_triage_batch_version.py>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_triage_batch_merge_git.py>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Phase 3: reservation reads the source from origin/<default> by key (proven against a real clone), follows every live reservation with the batch's bump; merge reconciles slots in merge order and re-versions with set in the scratch worktree (integration test against a local bare origin). After review r3-f2: a version-file conflict is taken from main only when the PR's own change to that file is the version alone (a lockfile only with a declared relock); a PR that also added a dependency to pyproject.toml stops the queue naming the file, the dependency intact, and an up-to-date PR off its slot is re-slotted against real git.<br>[super-fr:test_triage_batch_version.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_triage_batch_version.py>) [super-fr:test_triage_batch_merge.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_triage_batch_merge.py>) [super-fr:test_triage_batch_merge_git.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_triage_batch_merge_git.py>) |
| An operator can merge several batch PRs in a computed order that predicts shared-file conflicts up front; the queue stops on a real conflict naming the files, and a re-run resumes at the first unmerged PR | [super-fr:2026-09-25-triage-batches-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-25-triage-batches-design.md#3f-batch-merge>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_triage_batch_merge.py>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_triage_batch_merge_git.py>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Phase 3: merge order, printed plan, reconcile, refusals (draft, failing check, moved head, protection refusal verbatim, unsupported backend) unit-tested with the forge faked; execution against real git with a local bare origin, including resume. Live walk 2026-09-26 (Test Plan 21, phase 4) on derio-net/super-fr: the printed plan predicted the shared docs/acceptance files; the queue stopped on a real matrix.yaml conflict naming the file and pushing nothing; after a manual matrix union a re-run merged #652 at its reserved 4.23.2 and stopped on #651's matrix plus reports; after that union a re-run resumed at #651 and re-slotted it from 4.23.1 to 4.23.3 (a third version, #635, had taken 4.23.1 on main mid-flight), pushed, waited for checks and merged. The verb resolved no conflict in the four generated acceptance files: every super-fr batch pair conflicts there (structural fix in flight on feat/version-bump-churn).<br>[super-fr:test_triage_batch_merge.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_triage_batch_merge.py>) [super-fr:test_triage_batch_board.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_triage_batch_board.py>) [super-fr:test_triage_batch_merge_git.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_triage_batch_merge_git.py>) |

| An operator applies a changed devcontainer profile with `fr isolation rebuild`: the container is recreated with the new features while uncommitted worktree files and an active run's cursor survive (#577). | [super-fr:2026-09-23-lifecycle-container-vs-worktree-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-23-lifecycle-container-vs-worktree-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation_container_verbs.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Unit (every PR): rebuild argv = up argv + --remove-existing-container (+ --build-no-cache), missing worktree refused, changed old image reclaimed only on success (an already-gone one is not a warning, p6-f1), host no-op / external refuses, state + marker untouched. ## 64 · isolation verify-merge

| Acceptance | Origin | Levels | Automation | Evidence / notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| verify-merge accepts a merged branch whose added lines a later merge rewrote (its exact file content was on the base after the merge) and checks the fetched remote branch as well as the local one; unlanded content still refuses | [super-fr:2026-09-26-verify-merge-rewrites-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-verify-merge-rewrites-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Review fixes (super-fr#665 review): C1 perf (constant-cost archived-path blob scan), C2 correctness (verify_merge fetches the branch with an explicit refspec and requires branch_fetched), and S1-S5 spec/test corrections — also covers the run+usage archived kinds, a §C-only archive test, a usage re-capture test, and gc's _merged_by_content not classifying unlanded content as merged. super-fr#716: a merge later REVERTED no longer reads as landed — the blob fallback reads the path history oldest-first and a return to a pre-landing blob un-lands it (squash, rebase, synced-branch, revert-then-edit and revert-of-revert pinned). super-fr#741: a --no-ff merge-commit landing reverted with revert -m 1 reads missing (fork point = merge-base with the landing merge's first parent). super-fr#739: a three-way revert reads missing (the same log call reads each commit's patch; the branch's patch inverted un-lands). Known limit: a revert of a branch that only ADDED files stays indistinguishable from a later deletion or a consumed fragment. Still unit-only in tests/unit/test_isolation.py. super-fr#757: a fast-forward landing later reverted reads missing when fr recorded the branch's start at up (IsolationState.base_sha passed by verify-merge, gc and archive); with no surviving record it is indistinguishable, as before. super-fr#715: a squash whose blob combined a concurrent base edit, then rewritten, reads landed (the landing is recognised by its patch adding every line the branch added).<br>[super-fr:test_isolation.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_isolation.py>) |
